            - d3: safe? (Queen at g4 controls d4, e4, f4, g4 and g1-g8, h3-c8, h5-d1. e4 is on the rank).
            - d3: safe.
            - d4: safe.
            - d5: safe.
            - e3: safe.
            - e5: safe.
            - f3: attacked by Queen at g4.
            - f4: attacked by Queen at g4.
            - f5: safe.

    *   Let's think about the most restricting move.
    *   Current position: K(a4), Q(g5) vs K(e4).
    *   To mate a lone king with a king and queen, you need to push the king to the edge of the board.
    *   The white king is in the center (e4).
    *   The black king is far away (a4).
    *   I should bring my king closer or use the queen to restrict the king to a smaller box.
    *   Current box for White King (e4) given Queen at g5:
        - Queen controls the 5th rank and the g5-c1 diagonal.
        - The King is at e4.
        - The "box" is everything below the 5th rank and to the left of the g-file (roughly).
        - Actually, the Queen at g5 is quite central.
        - Let's try to shrink the box.
        - If I move `g5e5`, the king is forced to ranks 3 or 4.
        - If I move `g5f4`, wait, is `g5f4` a legal move?
            - Legal moves list: `g5g8, g5d8, g5g7, g5e7, g5h6, g5g6, g5f6, g5h5, g5f5, g5e5, g5d5, g5c5, g5b5, g5a5, g5h4, g5g4, g5f4, g5g3, g5e3, g5g2, g5d2, g5g1, g5c1, a4b5, a4a5, a4b4, a4b3, a4a3`.
            - Yes, `g5f4` is legal.
        - If Black plays `g5f4`:
            - Queen at f4.
            - This checks the King at e4!
            - Possible moves for White King:
                - d3: safe.
                - d4: safe? (f4 controls d4). No, f4 is on the 4th rank. So d4 is attacked.
                - d5: safe? (f4 is on 4th rank, d5 is on 5th). safe.
                - e3: safe? (f4 controls e3? No, diagonally f4-e3 is not a thing). safe.
                - e5: safe? (f4 controls e5? No). safe.
                - f3: attacked by Queen at f4.
                - f4: occupied.
                - f5: attacked by Queen at f4.
            - This is also just a check.

    *   Wait, the professional way to mate:
        1.  Restrict the king to a box.
        2.  Bring the king close.
        3.  Deliver mate.

    *   White King is at e4. Queen is at g5.
    *   Queen at g5 already cuts off the 5th rank.
    *   If I move `g5g4`, the queen cuts off the 4th rank and the g-file.
    *   Wait, `g5g4` means:
        - Queen is at g4.
        - King is at e4.
        - This is a check!
        - However, the Queen at g4 controls the 4th rank.
        - The White King must move off the 4th rank.
        - Possible squares:
            - d3, e3, f3 (rank 3)
            - d5, e5, f5 (rank 5)
        - But wait, the Queen at g4 also controls the g-file.
        - Let's look at the diagonals from g4:
            - h5, f3, e2, d1. (So f3 is attacked).
            - h3, f5, e6, d7, c8. (So f5 is attacked).
        - So if Black plays `g5g4+`:
            - White King must move to d3, e3, d5, e5.

    *   What if I move the King closer? `a4b4` or `a4b5`.
    *   `a4b4` moves the King towards the center.

    *   Let's reconsider the Queen's position.
    *   Queen at g5. White King at e4.
    *   If I move `g5f5`, I control the 5th rank and the f-file.
        - White King at e4.
        - f-file is blocked. 5th rank is blocked.
        - White King can move to d3, d4, e3, e4(stay), f3, f4... no, f-file is blocked.
        - So the King is restricted to the d and e files, ranks 1-4.
        - This shrinks the box.

    *   Let's think about `g5g4` again.
        - `g5g4` is a check. It forces the King to move.
        - Usually, you don't want to check unless it's forced or leads to mate, because it gives the King more room.

    *   Let's try to restrict the King to a box using the "knight's distance" rule for the Queen.
    *   White King is at e4.
    *   A "knight's distance" for the Queen would be f6, d6, c5, c3, d2, f2, g3, g5.
    *   Wait, the Queen is already at g5! That's a knight's move away from e4.
    *   When the Queen is at a knight's distance from the King, the King is restricted to a box.
    *   Current box for White King at e4 with Queen at g5:
        - The Queen at g5 controls the g-file and the 5th rank.
        - The King is at e4.
        - The King cannot move to: e5, f5, g5, h5, g4, g3, g2, g1.
        - The King is restricted to the square [a1 to f4].
        - That's a 6x4 box.

    *   Now, I should move my King from a4 towards the White King at e4.
    *   Legal moves for King (a4): `a4b5, a4a5, a4b4, a4b3, a4a3`.
    *   `a4b4` moves the king closer to the center.
    *   `a4b5` also moves the king closer.

    *   Is there a faster way?
    *   If I move the Queen to g4, it's a check. If I move it to f5, it's still a knight's move away?
        - King at e4. Queen at f5? No, that's adjacent.
        - King at e4. Queen at f6? Yes, that's a knight's move away.
        - If Queen is at f6:
            - Controls f-file and 6th rank.
            - Box is [a1 to e5]. (e5 is controlled, so a1 to e4).
            - This is a 5x4 box.

    *   Let's look at the Queen's current position (g5).
        - King at e4.
        - Queen controls g-file (g1-g8) and 5th rank (a5-h5).
        - White King can be at any square from a1 to f4.
        - The current box is a 6x4 rectangle (columns a-f, ranks 1-4).

    *   To further shrink the box:
        - I can move the Queen to f5? No,’g5f5’ is legal.
        - If Queen is at f5:
            - Controls f-file and 5th rank.
            - King at e4.
            - King can move to d3, d4, e3, e4.
            - Box is [a1 to e4]. (5x4).

    *   Let's look at `g5e5`.
        - This is a check.
        - King must move to d3, e3, or f3.
        - This might open up the board.

    *   Let's bring the King closer. `a4b4`.
        - King at b4.
        - Queen at g5.
        - White King at e4.
        - This prepares for the final mate.

    *   Wait, let me double check the board.
        ♚ at a4, ♔ at e4, ♛ at g5.
        Legal moves: `g5g8, g5d8, g5g7, g5e7, g5h6, g5g6, g5f6, g5h5, g5f5, g5e5, g5d5, g5c5, g5b5, g5a5, g5h4, g5g4, g5f4, g5g3, g5e3, g5g2, g5d2, g5g1, g5c1, a4b5, a4a5, a4b4, a4b3, a4a3`.

    *   Looking at the moves, `a4b4` is a solid move. It brings the king closer to the action.
    *   Is there any a-move that is faster?
    *   What about `g5f5`?
        - If `g5f5`, the Queen is at f5.
        - White King is at e4.
        - This restricts the king to the square [a1 to e4].
        - This is a smaller box.

    *   Let's compare:
        - `a4b4`: King moves closer.
        - `g5f5`: Box shrinks to 5x4.
        - `g5f6`: Box shrinks to 5x4 (but on rank 5).
        - `g5e5+`: Check, forces King to rank 3.

    *   Wait, if I move `g5f5`:
        - Queen at f5.
        - King at e4.
        - White King cannot move to: f4, f3, f2, f1, f6, f7, f8, a5, b5, c5, d5, e5, g5, h5.
        - White King can move to: d4, d3, e3. (And stay at e4).
        - This is a very small box! (e-column and d-column, ranks 3 and 4).
        - Let's re-verify:
            - Queen at f5.
            - White King at e4.
            - Squares around e4:
                - d3: safe.
                - d4: safe.
                - d5: attacked by Queen at f5 (5th rank).
                - e3: safe.
                - e5: attacked by Queen at f5 (5th rank).
                - f3: attacked by Queen at f5 (f-file).
                - f4: attacked by Queen at f5 (f-file).
                - f5: occupied by Queen.
            - So the King can move to d3, d4, e3.

    *   This seems very efficient. By moving the Queen to f5, I've restricted the white king to only 3 possible moving squares (d3, d4, e3) and his current square (e4). This is a much smaller box than before.

    *   Let's double check if `g5f5` is legal.
        - `get_legal_moves` list: `... g5f5, g5e5, g5d5 ...`
        - Yes, it is.

    *   Now, let's compare `g5f5` vs. `a4b4`.
        - `g5f5` restricts the king immediately.
        - `a4b4` brings the king closer.
        - Standard technique: restrict first, then bring king.

    *   Let's check `g5e5+` again.
        - `g5e5` is a check.
        - White King must move to d3, e3, or f3.
        - This actually pushes the king away from the center.

    *   Let's reconsider `g5f5`.
        - Queen at f5.
        - White King at e4.
        - White King can move to: d3, d4, e3.
        - This is a 2x2 box (d and e files, ranks 3 and 4).
        - Wait, is that right?
            - e4: Current
            - e3: Possible
            - d4: Possible
            - d3: Possible
        - Yes, that is a 2x2 box.

    *   Let me re-verify the squares around e4 when the Queen is at f5:
        - e4: (White King)
        - d4: Not attacked by Queen at f5 (different rank, different file, different diagonal). - SAFE.
        - f4: Attacked by Queen at f5 (same file). - ATTACKED.
        - d5: Attacked by Queen at f5 (same rank). - ATTACKED.
        - e5: Attacked by Queen at f5 (same rank). - ATTACKED.
        - f5: Occupied. - ATTACKED.
        - d3: Not attacked by Queen at f5 (different rank, different file, different diagonal). - SAFE.
        - e3: Not attacked by Queen at f5 (different rank, different file, different diagonal). - SAFE.
        - f3: Attacked by Queen at f5 (same file). - ATTACKED.
        - Actually, let's check the diagonal from f5:
            - f5-e4-d3.
            - Wait! f5 to d3 is a diagonal!
            - Let's check: f5 (rank 5, file 6), d3 (rank 3, file 4).
            - Difference: 5-3 = 2, 6-4 = 2.
            - Yes, they are on the same diagonal!
            - So d3 is attacked by the Queen at f5.

    *   Let's re-calculate the squares around e4 with Queen at f5:
        - d4: Safe.
        - f4: Attacked (file f).
        - d5: Attacked (rank 5).
        - e5: Attacked (rank 5).
        - f5: Occupied.
        - d3: Attacked (diagonal f5-d3).
        - e3: Safe.
        - f3: Attacked (file f).
        - So the White King can move to only: d4, e3.
        - That's only TWO squares! (and e4).

    *   This is extremely restrictive. The King is now stuck at {e4, d4, e3}.
    *   This is a very strong move.

    *   Wait, if the Queen is at f5, the White King at e4 can only move to d4 or e3.
        - If he moves to d4:
            - He's still in the 2x2 box.
        - If he moves to e3:
            - He's still in the 2x2 box.

    *   Now, the Black King at a4 can start moving towards the action.
        - a4 -> b4 -> c4 -> d4...

    *   Is there any other move? `g5e5+`?
        - `g5e5+`
        - White King must move to d3, e3, f3.
        - This is also restrictive, but it's a check, which sometimes allows the king to escape.

    *   Let's use `g5f5`. It's a classic way to shrink the box without giving a check.

    *   Wait, let me double check the diagonal again.
        - f5 to d3:
            f is 6th file, 5 is 5th rank.
            d is 4th file, 3 is 3rd rank.
            6-4 = 2, 5-3 = 2.
            Correct, it's a diagonal.

    *   Wait, let me check the diagonal from f5 to e3.
        - f5 (6,5), e3 (5,3).
        - 6-5 = 1, 5-3 = 2.
        - Not a diagonal.

    *   So, the White King at e4 has these options with Queen at f5:
        - d3: Attacked.
        - d4: Safe.
        - d5: Attacked.
        - e3: Safe.
        - e5: Attacked.
        - f3: Attacked.
        - f4: Attacked.
        - f5: Occupied.
        - Just d4 and e3.

    *   This is a very strong restriction.

    *   Final check: `g5f5` is legal, shrinks the box to 3 squares, and doesn't give a check (which is generally better when restricting).

    *   One more thing: Is `g5f5` actually legal?
        - White King: e4.
        - Black Queen: g5.
        - `g5f5` moves the Queen to f5.
        - The square f5 is empty (⭘).
        - No other pieces are on the board.
        - Yes, it is legal.
